# Mumbai

"Bombay" redirects here. For other uses, see [Bombay (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_\(disambiguation\)) and [Mumbai (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_\(disambiguation\)).

Not to be confused with [Bomb bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bomb_bay).

**Mumbai** ([/mʊmˈbaɪ/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [*muum-BY*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key); [Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language): *Mumbaī*, pronounced [\[ˈmumbəi\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Marathi) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/5/5f/Mumbai_Pronunciation.ogg/Mumbai_Pronunciation.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/5/5f/Mumbai_Pronunciation.ogg/Mumbai_Pronunciation.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup>), also known as **Bombay** ([/bɒmˈbeɪ/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [*bom-BAY*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key); its official name until 1995), is the [capital city](181337.md) of the Indian [state](375986.md) of [Maharashtra](20629.md) and the [financial capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_centre) of India. Located on the [Konkan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkan) coast along the [west coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Coastal_Plains) of India, it has a deep [natural harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_harbour). As per the [2011 Census of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Census_of_India), it is the [most populous city proper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_India_by_population) in India, with a population of almost 12.5 million, while the [Mumbai Metropolitan Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metropolitan_Region), which is among the [most populous metropolitan areas in the world](14649921.md), has a population of over 20 million. The city forms part of the [Mumbai City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_City_district) and [Mumbai Suburban districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Suburban_district) and is administered by the [Brihanmumbai Municipal Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brihanmumbai_Municipal_Corporation), which was established in 1888.

Mumbai, originally an [archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archipelago) of [seven islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_islands_of_Bombay), has evidence of human occupation dating back to the [South Asian Stone Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian_Stone_Age). The islands were ruled by the [Maurya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurya) Empire since the 3rd century BCE and were referenced by [Ptolemy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ptolemy) in his text in 150 CE. The islands were inhabited by the [Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language)–speaking [Koli people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koli_people), whose deity Mumbā is believed to have given the city its name. The region was later ruled by [several dynasties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Mumbai_under_indigenous_empires) such as [Satavahanas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satavahanas), [Western Satraps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Satraps), [Chalukyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chalukya_dynasty), [Rashtrakutas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashtrakuta_dynasty), and [Shilaharas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shilahara_dynasty), before coming under the control of the [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate), and later the [Gujarat Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarat_Sultanate) during the [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages). In 1535, the islands were ceded to the [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_India), and in 1661, transferred to the [British Crown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Crown) as part of [Catherine of Braganza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_of_Braganza)'s [dowry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dowry), before being leased to the [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company) in 1668.

Under [British Raj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj), Bombay was the capital of the [Bombay Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Presidency), and developed into a major trading and administrative centre. Beginning in 1782, Mumbai underwent major transformation through the [Hornby Vellard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hornby_Vellard) project, which involved the [reclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_reclamation) of land between the city's seven islands from the [Arabian Sea](2747.md). Alongside the construction of major [roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roads_in_India) and [railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_India), the reclamation project, completed in 1845, unified the islands and helped transform Mumbai into a major seaport on the Arabian Sea. This infrastructure expansion, combined with the 19th-century [cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton) boom, drove rapid economic growth and further established Mumbai as an important commercial centre. The city played a major role in the [Indian independence movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_independence_movement). After India's independence in 1947, Bombay became the capital of [Bombay State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_State) and after the [Samyukta Maharashtra movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samyukta_Maharashtra_movement), became the capital of [Maharashtra](20629.md) in 1960.

Mumbai is classified as an [alpha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network#Alpha) [world city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_city) and serves as India's financial, commercial, and entertainment capital, attracting migrants from across the country. It is often compared to [New York City](645042.md).[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24] In the early 2010s, the city contributed nearly five percent of India's [GDP](1472206.md), one-fourth of its industrial output, and one-third of its tax revenues. Being the capital of the state, it houses major government institutions including the [Maharashtra Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Legislative_Assembly), and the [Bombay High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_High_Court). It is home to major scientific, nuclear, and financial institutions, as well as the [headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_companies_headquartered_in_Mumbai) of numerous [Indian companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_companies) and [multinational corporations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multinational_corporations). It is also the centre of [Bollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollywood), one of the world's most prolific film industries, as well as [Marathi cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_cinema).

## Etymology

The name *Mumbai* ([Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language): मुंबई) originated from *Mumbā* or *Mahā-Ambā*, the name of the patron Hindu goddess ([Kula Devata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuladevata)) [Mumbadevi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbadevi) of the native [Koli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koli_people) community,[^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] and from *ā'ī*, meaning "mother" in Marathi.[^cite_note-27] By some accounts, the Koli community of [Kathiawar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathiawar) and [Central Gujarat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Gujarat) introduced their deity Mumba from Kathiawar, where her worship continues to this day.[^cite_note-28][^cite_note-29]



The [Mumba Devi Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumba_Devi_Temple), named for the goddess Mumba Devi, from whom the city of Mumbai may derive its name.

The oldest known names for the city are *Kakamuchee* and *Galajunkja*; these are sometimes still used.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPatelMasselos20034-30][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMehta2004130-31] Portuguese writer [Gaspar Correia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaspar_Correia) recorded the name "Bombaim" after 1512 in his *Lendas da Índia* (*Legends of India*).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShirodkar19984–5-32][Yule & Burnell 1996](#CITEREFYuleBurnell1996), p. [102](https://books.google.com/books?id=20pdFRekGvMC\&pg=PA102) Some [Anglophone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English-speaking_world) authors have suggested this came from a supposed [Galician–Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galician%E2%80%93Portuguese) phrase *bom baim*, "good little bay",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShirodkar19987-33] with no scientific basis.[^cite_note-Machado-34][^cite_note-35] In 1516, Portuguese explorer [Duarte Barbosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duarte_Barbosa) used the name *Tana-Maiambu* with *Tana* referring to the adjoining town of [Thane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thane) and *Maiambu* referring to *Mumbadevi* or the neighbouring island of [Mahim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahim).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShirodkar19982-36][^cite_note-37]

The form *Bombaim* is still commonly used in Portuguese. Many variant names were recorded in the 16th and the 17th centuries.[^cite_note-40] After the [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_overseas_possessions) gained possession of the city in the 17th century, the Portuguese name was [anglicised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglicise) to *Bombay*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGreater_Bombay_District_Gazetteer19606-41] Ali Muhammad Khan, imperial [dewan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dewan) or revenue minister of the Gujarat province, in the *Mirat-i Ahmedi* (1762) called the city *Manbai*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShirodkar19983-42]

The French traveller [Louis Rousselet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Rousselet), who visited in 1863 and 1868, stated in 1877 that "Etymologists have wrongly derived this name from the Portuguese Bôa Bahia, or (French: "bonne baie", English: "good bay"), not knowing that the tutelar goddess of this island has been, from remote antiquity, Bomba, or [Mumba Devi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumba_Devi_Temple), and that she still ... possesses a temple".[^cite_note-43] British officer and scholar [John Briggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Briggs_\(East_India_Company_officer\)) concurred that the name Bombay was a corruption of "Mumby", for a temple to Mumba Devi.[^cite_note-44]

By the late 20th century, the city was called *Mumbai* or *Mambai* in Marathi, [Konkani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkani_language), [Gujarati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarati_language), [Kannada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada_language) and [Sindhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sindhi_language), and *Bambai* in [Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi).[^cite_note-Beam-45] The Government of India officially changed the English name to *Mumbai* in November 1995.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHansen20011-46] This came at the insistence of the Marathi nationalist [Shiv Sena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiv_Sena_\(1966%E2%80%932022\)) party, which had just won the Maharashtra state elections, and mirrored [similar name changes across India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaming_of_cities_in_India).[^cite_note-47] Shiv Sena argued that the name 'Bombay' echoed British colonial rule.[^cite_note-Beam-45] While Mumbai is still called Bombay by some residents and by some Indians from other regions,[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49] mention of the city by a name other than *Mumbai* has become controversial.[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51]

### People from Mumbai

A resident of Mumbai is called *Mumbaikar* (pronounced [\[ˈmumbəikəɾ\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Marathi)) in Marathi, in which the suffix *-kar* means a 'resident of'. The term had been in use for quite some time, but it gained popularity after the official name change to Mumbai.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHansen20011-46] Older terms such as *Bombayite* are used infrequently.[^cite_note-52][^cite_note-53]

## History

Main article: [History of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Mumbai)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Mumbai).

### Early history

A white Buddhist stupa.

[Kanheri Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanheri_Caves) contain Buddhist artworks from the 1st century

Mumbai is built on what was originally an [archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archipelago) of [seven islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_islands_of_Bombay): [Isle of Bombay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isle_of_Bombay), [Parel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parel), [Mazagaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazagaon), [Mahim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahim), [Colaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colaba), [Worli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worli), and [Old Woman's Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Woman's_Island) (also known as *Little Colaba*).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFarooqui20061-54] [Pleistocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pleistocene) sediments near [Kandivali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kandivali) suggest that the islands were inhabited since the [South Asian Stone Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian_Stone_Age).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGhosh199025-55] In the 3rd century BCE, the islands became part of the [Maurya Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurya_Empire) under [Ashoka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashoka),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavid19955-56] followed by the [Satavahana dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satavahana_dynasty) in the 2nd century BCE.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGreater_Bombay_District_Gazetteer1960127–150-57] The Koli fishing community populated the region during the beginning of the [Common Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_Era).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGreater_Bombay_District_Gazetteer19605-58][^cite_note-59]

The [Mahakali Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahakali_Caves) in [Andheri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andheri) were cut out between the 1st century BCE and the 6th century CE,[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61] while the [Kanheri Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanheri_Caves) in [Borivali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borivali), excavated in the 1st century CE,[^cite_note-62] became an important centre of [Buddhism](3267529.md) in Western India.[^cite_note-63] In the 2nd century, Greek geographer [Ptolemy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ptolemy) referred to the islands as *Heptanesia* ([Ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek): A cluster of seven islands).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavid19738-64] Between the 2nd century and 9th centuries, the islands were ruled by successive [indigenous dynasties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Mumbai_under_indigenous_empires) including the [Western Satraps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Satraps), [Abhira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abhira_kingdom), [Vakataka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vakataka_dynasty), [Kalachuris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalachuris_of_Mahishmati), [Konkan Mauryas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mauryas_of_Puri), [Chalukyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chalukya_dynasty), and [Rashtrakutas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashtrakuta_dynasty),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGreater_Bombay_District_Gazetteer1960127–150-57] before being ruled by the [Shilaharas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shilahara) from 810 to 1260.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra200179-65] Important historic structures from this period that survive include the [Jogeshwari Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jogeshwari_Caves) (between 520 and 525),[^cite_note-66] [Elephanta Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elephanta_Caves) (between 6th to 7th century),[^cite_note-67] [Walkeshwar Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walkeshwar_Temple) (10th century),[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69] and [Banganga Tank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banganga_Tank) (12th century).[^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71]

In the late 13th century, [King Bhima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhima_of_Mahikavati) established a kingdom in the region and made Mahikawati (present-day [Mahim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahim)) its capital.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra200151-72] Around 1298, the [Pathare Prabhus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pathare_Prabhu), among the earliest known settlers of the city, migrated to Mahikawati from [Saurashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saurashtra_\(region\)) in Gujarat.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaharashtra20041703-73] The [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate) annexed the islands in 1347–48 and ruled them until 1407, during which they were administered by [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim) governors of Gujarat appointed by the sultanate.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavid197314-74][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavid199512-75] The islands were subsequently governed by the independent [Gujarat Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarat_Sultanate), established in 1407. Numerous mosques were built during the period, including the [Haji Ali Dargah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haji_Ali_Dargah) in [Worli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worli), erected in 1431 in honour of the [Sufi saint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufi_saint) Haji Ali.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhalidi200624-76] From 1429 to 1431, the islands were contested between the Gujarat Sultanate and the [Bahmani Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahmani_Sultanate) of the [Deccan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMisra1982193,_222-77] and in 1493, [Bahadur Khan Gilani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahadur_Khan_Gilani) of the Bahmani Sultanate attempted to conquer them but was repulsed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavid197316-78]

### Portuguese and British rule

Main articles: [History of Bombay under Portuguese rule (1534–1661)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Bombay_under_Portuguese_rule_\(1534%E2%80%931661\)) and [History of Bombay under British rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Bombay_under_British_rule)



[Madh Fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madh_Fort), built by the [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_India)

Apprehensive of the power of the [Mughal Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire) under [Humayun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humayun), Gujarat Sultan [Bahadur Shah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahadur_Shah_of_Gujarat) signed the [Treaty of Bassein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Bassein_\(1534\)) with the [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Empire) on 23 December 1534, through which the seven islands of Bombay, along with [Bassein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasai) and its dependencies, were ceded to the Portuguese on 25 October 1535.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGreater_Bombay_District_Gazetteer1960166-79] During Portuguese rule, the islands were leased to Portuguese officials, and the name Bombaim came into use.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGreater_Bombay_District_Gazetteer1960169-80] [Roman Catholic](606848.md) religious orders, particularly [Franciscans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franciscans) and [Jesuits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuits), established several churches such as [St. Michael's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Michael's_Church_\(Mumbai\)) at Mahim (1534),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavid199519-81] [St. John the Baptist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._John_the_Baptist_Church,_Mumbai) at Andheri (1579),[^cite_note-82] [St. Andrew's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Andrew's_Church_\(Mumbai\)) at [Bandra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandra) (1580),[^cite_note-83] and [Gloria Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloria_Church) at [Byculla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byculla) (1632).[^cite_note-84] They also constructed several fortifications including the [Bombay Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Castle), [Castella de Aguada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castella_de_Aguada), and [Madh Fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madh_Fort).[^cite_note-85] Recognising Bombay's strategic natural harbour, the [British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire) competed with the Portuguese for control of the islands.[^cite_note-86] On 11 May 1661, the islands were transferred by the Portuguese to the English as part of [Catherine of Braganza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_of_Braganza)'s [dowry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dowry) upon her marriage to [Charles II of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_II_of_England).[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88]



English Fort in Bombay, c. 1665

Under the [Royal Charter of 27 March 1668](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Charter_of_27_March_1668), the English Crown leased these islands to the [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company) for an annual rent of [£](270673.md)10.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra200120-89] Under the Company rule, the population grew rapidly from about 10,000 in 1661 to 60,000 by 1675.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavid1973410-90] However, the islands faced several attacks and incursions by [Yakut Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yakut_Khan), the Koli admiral of the Mughal Empire, in October 1672,[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93] [Rickloffe van Goen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rickloffe_van_Goen), the governor-general of [Dutch India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company) on 20 February 1673,[^cite_note-94] and [Siddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siddi) admiral [Sambal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siddi_Sambal) on 10 October 1673.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYimene200494-95] In 1687, the East India Company transferred its headquarters from [Surat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surat) to Bombay, and it later later became the headquarters of the [Bombay Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Presidency) in 1662.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECarsten1961427-96][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavid1973179-97] It continued to face incursions, including another attack by Yakut Khan in 1689–90.[^cite_note-98]

Portuguese control in the region ended when the [Marathas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_Confederacy) under [Peshwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peshwa) [Baji Rao I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baji_Rao_I) captured [Salsette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salsette_Island) in 1737, and Bassein in 1739.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHistory_of_Medieval_India126-99] By the mid-18th century, Bombay had developed into a major trading centre, attracting migrants from across the Indian subcontinent.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra200132-100] The British occupied Salsette on 28 December 1774, and through the [Treaty of Surat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Surat) (1775), sought to secure control of Salsette and Bassein, contributing to the outbreak of the [First Anglo-Maratha War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Anglo-Maratha_War). [^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFortescue2008145-101] British control of Salsette was officially confirmed through the Treaty of Purandar (1776) and the Treaty of Salbai (1782), which formally settled the conflict.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENaravane200756,_63-102]



Ships at [Bombay Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Harbour) (c. 1731)

From 1782, the British undertook major engineering works to unite Bombay's seven islands by means of a [causeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Causeway), the [Hornby Vellard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hornby_Vellard), completed by 1784.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra200128-103][^cite_note-104][^cite_note-105] In 1817, the East India Company under [Mountstuart Elphinstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountstuart_Elphinstone) defeated [Baji Rao II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baji_Rao_II), the last Peshwa of the Marathas in the [Battle of Khadki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Khadki),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENaravane200780–82-106] ending the resistance from native powers and established British control over the Deccan and the Bombay Presidency.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGreater_Bombay_District_Gazetteer1960233-107] As Bombay's economic importance grew rapidly, connectivity was improved and India's first passenger railway opened between Bombay and [Thana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thane) on 16 April 1853.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra2001127-108] During the [American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War) (1861–1865), the disruption of cotton supplies from the United States transformed the city into a major cotton-trading centre globally, resulting in a significant economic boom.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra2001343-109] The opening of the [Suez Canal](29323.md) in 1869 transformed Bombay into one of the largest seaports on the [Arabian Sea](2747.md) and enhanced its strategic importance.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra200188-110] However, the city was [hit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_plague_epidemic) by a [bubonic plague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bubonic_plague) epidemic that killed more than 20,000 people and caused many residents to flee,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra200174-111] significantly affecting the textile industry and its economy.[^cite_note-112] In the 20th century, Bombay also became an important centre of the [Indian independence movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_independence_movement), hosting the 1942 session of the [Indian National Congress](149333.md) that launched the [Quit India Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quit_India_Movement), and the [Royal Indian Navy Mutiny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Indian_Navy_Mutiny) of 1946.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra2001293,_345-113]

### Independent India

Main article: [History of Bombay in Independent India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Bombay_in_Independent_India)



[Municipal Corporation Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Corporation_Building,_Mumbai) (center) with the [Victoria Terminus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Terminus) (right) in 1950

After India's independence in 1947, the Bombay Presidency was reorganised into [Bombay State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_State), which was later expanded through the integration of several [princely states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princely_states), with Bombay serving as its capital.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus_of_India196123-114] In April 1950, the [Mumbai suburban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_suburban_district) and [city districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_city_district) were merged to create the [Greater Bombay Municipal Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brihanmumbai_Municipal_Corporation).[^cite_note-115] During the 1950s, the [Samyukta Maharashtra movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samyukta_Maharashtra_movement) intensified, demanding a separate Marathi-speaking state with Bombay as its capital. However, debate continued over the city's status, with the Congress advocating for Bombay to become an autonomous city-state in 1955.[^cite_note-116] While the [States Reorganisation Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_Reorganisation_Act) proposed a bilingual state of Maharashtra–[Gujarat](53707.md) with Bombay as its capital in 1955, the [Bombay Citizens' Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Citizens'_Committee), representing [Gujarati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarati_people) industrialists, lobbied for an independent status for the city.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuha2007197–198-117]

A stone statue of torch-bearers as seen at night. A fountain with a white base is in the background

The [Hutatma Chowk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hutatma_Chowk) memorial honours the martyrs of the [Samyukta Maharashtra Samiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samyukta_Maharashtra_Samiti)

Following widespread protests in which 105 people died in clashes with [law enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_enforcement), Bombay State was reorganised along linguistic lines on 1 May 1960, creating the separate states of Maharashtra and Gujarat.[^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119] The [Gujarati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarati_language)-speaking regions became Gujarat, while Maharashtra was formed by combining the Marathi-speaking areas of Bombay State with territories from [Central Provinces and Berar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Provinces_and_Berar), [Hyderabad State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyderabad_State), and other princely states, with Bombay as its capital.[^cite_note-120][^cite_note-121] The Flora Fountain was renamed Hutatma Chowk (Martyr’s Square), and a memorial was erected in memory of those who died during the Samyukta Maharashtra movement.[^cite_note-122]

Since the 1960s, Bombay experienced rapid expansion with areas such as [Nariman Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nariman_Point) and [Cuffe Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuffe_Parade) were reclaimed from the sea and developed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDwivediMehrotra2001306-123] Industrialisation expanded the [petrochemicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrochemicals), [electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronics), and [automobiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automobiles) sectors. Major petroleum refineries such as the [Mumbai Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Refinery_\(HPCL\)) at [Trombay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trombay) and [BPCL Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Refinery_\(BPCL\)) were established.[^cite_note-124] To coordinate the growing metropolitan region, the [Government of Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Maharashtra) established the [Bombay Metropolitan Region Development Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metropolitan_Region_Development_Authority) in 1975 to oversee planning and development across the city and its surrounding areas.[^cite_note-125] In 1979, [New Bombay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Bombay) was established by the [City and Industrial Development Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_and_Industrial_Development_Corporation) from parts of [Thane](638817.md) and [Raigad districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raigad_district) as a planned [satellite township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satellite_township) to accommodate Mumbai's rapidly growing population. The [Great Bombay Textile Strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Bombay_Textile_Strike), which involved nearly 250,000 workers across more than 50 mills, resulted in the shutdown of several textile mills,[^cite_note-126] which were [redeveloped](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redevelopment_of_Mumbai_mills) later.[^cite_note-127][^cite_note-128] On 26 May 1989, the [Jawaharlal Nehru Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru_Port) was commissioned at [Nhava Sheva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nhava_Sheva) to relieve congestion at the [Mumbai Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Harbour).[^cite_note-129] In 1990, Greater Bombay was administratively divided into separate city and suburban revenue districts, although both continued to be governed by the same municipal administration.[^cite_note-130]



Skyline of Mumbai in the early 21st century

During the 1990s and 2000s, Bombay experienced a series of violent events and terrorist attacks. Following the [demolition of the Babri Masjid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demolition_of_the_Babri_Masjid) in [Ayodhya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayodhya), the city witnessed severe [Hindu-Muslim riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_riots) in 1992–93, resulting in more than 1,000 deaths.[^cite_note-131] In March 1993, [13 coordinated bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Bombay_bombings) across the city by [Islamic extremists](8734632.md) and the [Mumbai underworld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_underworld) killed 257 people and injured more than 700.[^cite_note-132] The 2000s saw further terrorist incidents such as the [2006 Mumbai train bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Mumbai_train_bombings) killed 209 people and injured over 700,[^cite_note-133] and the [2008 Mumbai attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Mumbai_attacks) that resulted in 173 deaths and 308 injuries over three days, and caused extensive damage to major hotels and heritage landmarks.[^cite_note-134] In July 2011, three coordinated [bomb blasts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Mumbai_bombings) at the [Opera house](155040.md), [Zaveri Bazaar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaveri_Bazaar) and [Dadar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dadar) killed 26 people and injured 130.[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136] Despite these setbacks, Mumbai continued to grow as a major economic centre in the 21st century. It emerged as India's commercial and financial capital and a global financial hub, becoming home to major financial services companies and a focus of infrastructure development and private investment.[^cite_note-Mumbai_global-137][^cite_note-138] It grew into one of South Asia's largest cities and is the home of [Bollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollywood), one of the world's most prolific film industries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrunnWilliamsZeigler2003353–354-139]

## Geography

Main articles: [Geography of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Mumbai) and [Geology of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_Mumbai)

See also: [Eastern Suburbs (Mumbai)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Suburbs_\(Mumbai\)), [Western Suburbs (Mumbai)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Suburbs_\(Mumbai\)), and [List of neighbourhoods in Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_neighbourhoods_in_Mumbai)



Mumbai consists of two districts, [Mumbai City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_City_district) and [Mumbai Suburban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Suburban_district)

Mumbai is spread over an area of 603 km<sup>2</sup> (233 sq mi),[^cite_note-140] and is divided into two districts–[Mumbai City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_City_district) and [Mumbai Suburban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Suburban_district).[^cite_note-141] The suburban district occupies 446 km<sup>2</sup> (172 sq mi) and is divided into three [tehsils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehsils)–[Andheri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andheri), [Borivali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borivali), and [Kurla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurla).[^cite_note-142] The city district-region located to the south is referred to as [South Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Mumbai) or "Island City", and occupies an area of 157 km<sup>2</sup> (61 sq mi).[^cite_note-mmrda_muip_gdp-143][^cite_note-144] The [Mumbai Metropolitan Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metropolitan_Region) includes portions of [Thane](638817.md), [Palghar](43059179.md) and [Raigad districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raigad_district) in addition to Greater Mumbai, and covers an area of 4,355 km<sup>2</sup> (1,681 sq mi).[^cite_note-projectsecoa1-7]



A 2016 [satellite image](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satellite_image) of Mumbai

Mumbai is on a narrow [peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peninsula) located on the [Salsette Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salsette_Island), which is bound by the [Arabian Sea](2747.md) to the west, [Thane Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thane_Creek) to the east, and [Vasai Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasai_Creek) to the north.[^cite_note-145][^cite_note-mloc-146] It lies at the mouth of the [Ulhas River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulhas_River) on the [western coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Coastal_Plains) of India, in the [Konkan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkan) region.[^cite_note-geo-147] It shares the Salsette Island, with the Thane district. [Navi Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navi_Mumbai) is located to the east of the Thane Creek, and [Thane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thane) is situated towards the north of the Vasai Creek.[^cite_note-148]

The majority of the city lies just above the [mean sea level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mean_sea_level), with elevations ranging from 10 to 15 m (33 to 49 ft),[^cite_note-149] and an average elevation of 14 m (46 ft).[^cite_note-Wbase-150] Northern Mumbai consists of hilly terrain,[^cite_note-151] with the highest point in the city at 450 m (1,480 ft) in the [Powai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powai)–[Kanheri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanheri) range.[^cite_note-152] The coastline is indented with [creeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creek_\(tidal\)) and bays, stretching from the Thane creek in the east to Madh Marve on the western front.[^cite_note-153] The eastern coast of the Salsette Island is covered with [mangrove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangrove) [swamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swamp), while the western coast is mostly sandy and rocky.[^cite_note-154]

Soil cover in the city region is predominantly sandy. In the suburbs, the soil cover is largely [alluvial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alluvial_soil) and [loamy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loamy_soil).[^cite_note-155] The underlying rock is black [Deccan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau) basalt, dating to the late [Cretaceous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cretaceous) and early [Eocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eocene) eras.[^cite_note-156] Mumbai sits on a [seismically active](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seismicity) zone with 23 [fault lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fault_line).[^cite_note-157] The area is a [Seismic Zone III region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earthquake_hazard_zoning_of_India),[^cite_note-158] which means an earthquake of up to magnitude 6.5 on the [Richter magnitude scale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richter_magnitude_scale) could occur.[^cite_note-159]

The [Sanjay Gandhi National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanjay_Gandhi_National_Park) (Borivali National Park) is situated partly in the Mumbai suburban and Thane districts, and covers an area of 103.09 km<sup>2</sup> (39.80 sq mi).[^cite_note-160] There are two major lakes in the city– [Tulsi Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulsi_Lake) and [Vihar Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vihar_Lake). The city also draws its water supply from various dams located in Thane district including [Bhatsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhatsa_Dam), [Lower Vaitarna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_Vaitarna_Dam), [Middle Vaitarna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Vaitarna_Dam), [Tansa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tansa_Dam), and [Upper Vaitarna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Vaitarna_Dam).[^cite_note-161] The [Powai Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powai_Lake) is an artificial lake within the city limits, is used only for agricultural and industrial purposes.[^cite_note-162] The [Mithi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mithi_River) is an urban river formed by the [tailwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tailwater) discharges of the Powai and Vihar lakes, and is highly polluted.[^cite_note-163]

### Climate

Main article: [Climate of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Mumbai)

See also: [Mumbai Climate Action Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Climate_Action_Plan)

| Mumbai |

| Imperial conversion JFMAMJJASOND       0     88 63       0     90 64       0     91 70       0     93 75       0.5     93 81       21     90 79       34     86 77       25     86 77       13     88 77       3.7     91 75       0.6     93 70       0.2     90 66   █ Average max. and min. temperatures in °F █ Precipitation totals in inches |

Mumbai has a [tropical wet and dry climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_savanna_climate), classified as *Aw* under the [Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification). The central and northern suburbs have a [tropical monsoon climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_monsoon_climate) (*Am*) with heavier rainfall during the wet season. Mumbai has a nearly rainless stretch from October to May, with its most intense rainfall during July.[^cite_note-165] A cooler season from December to February is followed by a hotter season from March to May. The period from June to the end of September is the [southwest monsoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monsoon_of_Indian_subcontinent) season.[^cite_note-166]

The average annual temperature is 27 °C (81 °F).[^cite_note-Rohli_2007_267-167] In the city, the average maximum and minimum temperatures are 31 °C (88 °F) and 24 °C (75 °F) respetively. In the suburbs, the daily mean maximum temperature ranges from 29 °C (84 °F) to 33 °C (91 °F). The daily mean minimum temperature ranges from 16 °C (61 °F) to 26 °C (79 °F).[^cite_note-clra-168] The record high is 42.2 °C (108.0 °F) set on 14 April 1952.[^cite_note-IMDextremes-169] The record low was 7.4 °C (45.3 °F), set on 27 January 1962.[^cite_note-IMDextremes-169][^cite_note-170] The average annual [precipitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precipitation_\(meteorology\)) is 2,213 mm (87.1 in).[^cite_note-Rohli_2007_267-167] The maximum annual rainfall ever recorded was 3,452 mm (136 in) for 1954.[^cite_note-clra-168] The [most rainfall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Mumbai_floods) recorded in a single day was 944 mm (37 in) on 26 July 2005.[^cite_note-171] The average total annual rainfall is 2,213.4 mm (87 in) in Mumbai City and 2,502.3 mm (99 in) for the suburbs.[^cite_note-clra-168]

&#x20;Mumbai encounters occasional [tropical cyclones](8282374.md). The worst [cyclone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyclone) encountered was on 23 November 1948, with gusts reaching 151 km/h (94 mph), and resulted in 38 deaths and 47 people missing.[^cite_note-172][^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174] The city is prone to [flooding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_flooding) during monsoons,[^cite_note-175][^cite_note-176] Unplanned drainage systems and [informal settlements](63298152.md) are amongst the key drivers of Mumbai's frequent floods.[^cite_note-WEF-177] The [2005 Mumbai floods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Mumbai_floods) caused 500-1000 deaths and financial losses of US\$ 1.2 billion.[^cite_note-WEF-177][^cite_note-david,david\&Janel-178] To reduce flooding in Mumbai, the [Maharashtra government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_government) implemented a flood mitigation plan that included restoring the Mithi River, restructuring the drainage system, and moving informal settlements.[^cite_note-WEF-177][^cite_note-david,david\&Janel-178][^cite_note-LSC-179]

### Pollution

[Air pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_pollution_in_India) is a major issue in Mumbai.[^cite_note-183][^cite_note-184][^cite_note-185] In 2013, the annual average [PM2.5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PM2.5) concentration was 63 μg/m<sup>3</sup>, which was 6.3 times higher than that recommended by the [WHO Air Quality Guideline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WHO_Air_Quality_Guideline) for annual mean PM2.5.[^cite_note-186][^cite_note-guidelines-187] The [Central Pollution Control Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Pollution_Control_Board) of the [Government of India](553883.md) monitors and publicly shares real-time air quality data.[^cite_note-188] In December 2019, the [Indian Institute of Technology Bombay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Technology_Bombay), in partnership with the [McKelvey School of Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McKelvey_School_of_Engineering) at [Washington University in St. Louis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_University_in_St._Louis), launched the Aerosol and Air Quality Research Facility at Mumbai to study air pollution in Indian cities.[^cite_note-189] According to the 2016 report of the Central Pollution Control Board, Mumbai is the noisiest city in India.[^cite_note-190]

## Demographics

See also: [Growth of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Growth_of_Mumbai)

| Historical population |  |
| --- | --- |
| YearPop.±%1872 644,405—    1881 773,196+20.0%1891 821,764+6.3%1901 812,912−1.1%1911 1,018,388+25.3%1921 1,244,934+22.2%1931 1,268,306+1.9%1941 1,686,127+32.9% | YearPop.±%1951 2,966,902+76.0%1961 4,152,056+39.9%1971 5,970,575+43.8%1981 8,243,405+38.1%1991 9,925,891+20.4%2001 11,914,398+20.0%2011 12,442,373+4.4% |
| Based on Census data of the [Government of India](553883.md) Source: [MMRDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metropolitan_Region_Development_Authority)[^cite_note-191] |  |

In the [2011 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_census_of_India), Mumbai had a population of 12,442,373 inhabitants, with an estimated population density of about 20,482/km<sup>2</sup> (53,050/sq mi) and averate living space of 4.5 m<sup>2</sup> (48 sq ft) per person.[^cite_note-Census-192][^cite_note-193] The Mumbai Metropolitan Region was home to 18,394,912 people in 2011.[^cite_note-extended_UA_2011-9] The [sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_ratio) as 853 females per 1,000 males, lower than the national average of 914 females per 1,000 males. The child sex ratio was 913 females per 1000 males.[^cite_note-Census-192]

The low sex ratio is partly because of the large number of male migrants who come to the city to work.[^cite_note-194] The city had a literacy rate of 89.73%, higher than the national average of 86.7%.[^cite_note-Census-192] There were 4.2 million households in 2008, which was projected to increase to 6.6 million by 2020. The number of households with annual incomes of above 2 million rupees was projected to be about 10%, and households with incomes from 1 to 2 million is estimated to be 15% in 2020.[^cite_note-195]

As per the 2011 census, there were 5,633,709 slum-dwellers living in 1,135,514 households.[^cite_note-Census-192][^cite_note-196][^cite_note-197] Dharavi, located in central Mumbai, is the largest slum and houses about a million people in an area of 2.39 km<sup>2</sup> (0.92 sq mi), making it one of the most densely populated areas on Earth.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199][^cite_note-Town-200] The number of migrants to Mumbai from outside Maharashtra during the 1991–2001 decade was 1.12 million, which amounted to 54.8% of the net addition to the population of Mumbai.[^cite_note-201]

### Ethnic groups and religions

Further information: [Religion in Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Maharashtra)

| Religion |  | Percent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Maharashtra) |  | 65.99% |
| [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Maharashtra) |  | 20.65% |
| [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_Buddhists) |  | 4.85% |
| [Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism_in_Maharashtra) |  | 4.10% |
| [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Maharashtra) |  | 3.27% |
| [Sikhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikhism_in_Maharashtra) |  | 0.49% |
| Other or not stated |  | 0.63% |

As per the 2011 Census, [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) is the major religion with nearly two-thirds of the population adhering to it, followed by [Islam](6037917.md), [Buddhism](3267529.md), [Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism), [Christianity](5211.md), and [Sikhism](27964.md).[^cite_note-Census-192][^cite_note-Religion-202] The oldest Muslim communities in Mumbai include the [Dawoodi Bohras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dawoodi_Bohra), Ismaili [Khojas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khojas), and [Konkani Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkani_Muslims).[^cite_note-203] Native Christians include [East Indian Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_East_Indians), converted by the [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_India) during the 16th century.[^cite_note-204]

[Jews settled in Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Mumbai) during the 18th century. The [Bene Israeli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bene_Israel) Jewish community of Mumbai, who migrated from the [Konkan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konkan) villages, south of Mumbai, are believed to be the descendants of the Jews of Israel shipwrecked off the Konkan coast, probably in the year 175 BCE, during the reign of the [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greece) ruler, [Antiochus IV Epiphanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antiochus_IV_Epiphanes).[^cite_note-205] Mumbai is home to the largest population of [Parsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parsi) [Zoroastrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoroastrian) in the world,[^cite_note-206] numbering about 60,000, however their population is declining rapidly.[^cite_note-207] Parsis migrated to India from [Greater Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Iran) following the [Muslim conquest of Persia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_conquest_of_Persia) in the seventh century.[^cite_note-208] The ethnic demographics in the Mumbai consists of [Maharashtrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtrians) (32%), [Gujaratis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujaratis) (20%), with the rest hailing from other parts of India.[^cite_note-209]

### Language

| Language |  | Percent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language) |  | 35.4% |
| [Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi) |  | 24.8% |
| [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) |  | 11.7% |
| [Gujarati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarati_language) |  | 11.4% |
| [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language) |  | 2.37% |
| Others |  | 14.5% |

[Marathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_language) is the most spoken language in the city. [Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindi) is the second–most-spoken language, followed by [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) and [Gujarati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarati_language).[^cite_note-census2011-langreport-210][^cite_note-212][^cite_note-51st_Report_CLMI-213] Marathi and English serve as the official languages of the government.[^cite_note-51st_Report_CLMI-213] English is the principal language of the city's [white collar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-collar_worker) workforce. A colloquial form of Hindi, known as *[Bombay Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Hindi)* is spoken on the streets.[^cite_note-214] Many Hindi speakers are workers from [Uttar Pradesh](231623.md) and [Bihar](442482.md) who migrate seasonally to Mumbai to work as labourers.[^cite_note-215]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Mumbai)



Skyscrapers on [Altamount Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altamount_Road)

Mumbai is known as the financial and commercial capital of India,[^cite_note-Mumbai_global-137][^cite_note-216] generating about five percent of the country's total GDP.[^cite_note-mmrda_muip_gdp-143][^cite_note-The_Financial_Express-217] In 2023–24, it contributed to 19.8% of the GDP of the state of Maharashtra.[^cite_note-218] In the early 2010s, the city contributed to one-third of the total tax revenues and nearly one-fourth of the industrial output of the country.[^cite_note-NMIAtender-219][^cite_note-220] In 2017–18, estimates of the economy of the [Mumbai Metropolitan Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metropolitan_Region) ranged from \$368 billion to \$400 billion ([PPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity) & [metro GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_metropolitan_product)), ranking it amongst the top two most-productive [metro area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_GDP) of India.[^cite_note-Mumbai_GDP-221][^cite_note-GQ-222] In 2006, Mumbai employed 10% of the nation's factory workforce, contributed to 25% of the industrial output, 33% of the [income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_tax) collections, 60% of the [customs duty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Customs_\(tax\)) collections, 20% of the central [excise tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Excise_tax) collections, 40% of the [foreign trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_trade), and generated ₹40 [billion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1,000,000,000) (equivalent to ₹140 billion or US\$1.5 billion in 2026) in [corporate tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporate_tax).[^cite_note-223]



The [Bombay Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Stock_Exchange) is the oldest in Asia

Until the 1970s, Mumbai owed its prosperity largely to [textile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textile) mills and [seaport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seaport), but the local economy has since then diversified to include [finance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finance), [engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engineering), diamond-polishing, [healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare), and information technology.[^cite_note-224] It witnessed an economic boom since the [liberalisation of 1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_liberalisation_in_India), with the finance sector booming in the mid-nineties, and the [information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology) (IT), export, services, and outsourcing booming in the 2000s.[^cite_note-225] In the 21st century. the key sectors contributing to the city's economy are: finance, gems & jewellery, leather processing, IT and [ITES](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_process_outsourcing), textiles, petrochemical, electronics manufacturing, automobiles, and entertainment.[^cite_note-ecoprofile-226] The port and shipping industry is well established.[^cite_note-227] [Dharavi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharavi), in central Mumbai, has a large recycling industry and is home to about 15,000 single-room factories.[^cite_note-gua-228]

The city is home to many of India's conglomerates and five of the [Fortune Global 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_Global_500) companies.[^cite_note-Mumbai_global-137] It also houses the [Reserve Bank of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_Bank_of_India) , the [Bombay Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Stock_Exchange) (BSE), the [National Stock Exchange of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Stock_Exchange_of_India), and financial sector regulators such as the [Securities and Exchange Board of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Securities_and_Exchange_Board_of_India). [Nariman Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nariman_Point) and [Bandra Kurla Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandra_Kurla_Complex) are Mumbai's major financial centres.[^cite_note-ecoprofile-226] The BSE, established in 1875, is the oldest [stock exchange](28168.md) in Asia.[^cite_note-229] In 2019, Mumbai was among the [world's top ten cities by number of billionaires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_number_of_billionaires).[^cite_note-Mumbai_data-230] With a total wealth of around \$960 billion,[^cite_note-GQ-222][^cite_note-231] it is the wealthiest Indian city and one of the richest cities in the world.[^cite_note-Mumbai_info-232] As of 2008, the [Globalization and World Cities Research Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network) has ranked Mumbai as an "[alpha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network#Alpha) world city", third in its categories of [Global cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city).[^cite_note-lboro2008-233] Mumbai is the third most expensive office market in the world, and has been ranked among the fastest cities in the country for business startup.[^cite_note-World_Bank_and_International_Financial_Corporation-234]



[Dharavi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharavi) is one of the largest slums in the world

Mumbai is one of the world's most unequal cities in terms of wealth distribution and inclusivity.[^cite_note-235] According to the [World Bank](36752.md), in 2010, the median income was ₹20,000 (equivalent to ₹49,000 or US\$510 in 2026) while the average income was ₹40,000 (equivalent to ₹98,000 or US\$1,000 in 2026).[^cite_note-236] The city has a large unskilled and semi-skilled self-employed population, and other [blue collar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_collar) professions, often migrating from other regions.[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238] About 41.8% of the city's population live in urban slums, though slums occupy just around seven per cent of the city's total land area.[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-Slum-240]

Mumbai's overall per capita residential area is 8.3 m<sup>2</sup> (89 sq ft). In the city's slums, it drops to 2.73 m<sup>2</sup> (29.4 sq ft).[^cite_note-Slum-240] Dharavi hosts nearly a million people, and is one of the largest slums in the world.[^cite_note-241] The median rental cost of a one-bedroom apartment in Mumbai proper was about ₹30,000 (equivalent to ₹41,000 or US\$420 in 2026) in 2019.[^cite_note-Lewis-242] With available land at a premium, Mumbai residents often endure cramped housing, far from workplaces, with long commutes on crowded mass transits or roads clogged with traffic. Suburban residents spend a significant amount of time commuting southward to the central commercial district.[^cite_note-243]

## Government and politics

### Civic administration



Headquarters of the [Brihanmumbai Municipal Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brihanmumbai_Municipal_Corporation) (BMC), the largest civic organisation in the country.

Greater Mumbai (or Brihanmumbai), an area of 603 km<sup>2</sup> (233 mi<sup>2</sup>),[^cite_note-244] consisting of the [Mumbai City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_City_district) and [Mumbai Suburban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Suburban_district) districts, extends from [Colaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colaba) in the south, to [Mulund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulund) and [Dahisar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dahisar) in the north, and [Mankhurd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mankhurd) in the east. Its population as per the 2011 census was 12,442,373.[^cite_note-census2011-gtmumcity-245]

It is administered by the [Brihanmumbai Municipal Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brihanmumbai_Municipal_Corporation) (BMC), also referred to as the [Municipal Corporation of Greater Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Corporation_of_Greater_Mumbai), and formerly known as the Bombay Municipal Corporation.[^cite_note-mloc-146] The BMC is in charge of the civic and infrastructure needs of the metropolis.[^cite_note-mcmmm-246] BMC is the richest civic body in India and among the wealthiest in Asia.[^cite_note-247] As per the [Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Housing_and_Urban_Affairs), the BMC had a revenue of ₹378.91 billion (US\$3.9 billion) and an expenditure of ₹338.25 billion (US\$3.5 billion) in 2022–23. Taxes contributed to 17.1% of the revenue, with 51.2% of the income coming from other sources, and 31.8% from government grants.[^cite_note-248]

The BMC is headed by a mayor, who serves for a term of two and a half years, is chosen through an [indirect election](652583.md) by the councillors from among themselves.[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-mayorterm-250] The [municipal commissioner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Commissioner_of_Mumbai) is the chief executive officer and head of the executive arm of the municipal corporation. The commissioner, who is an [Indian Administrative Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Administrative_Service) officer appointed by the [state government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Maharashtra), is responsible for implementing the executive policies. The commissioner is appointed for a fixed term, and the powers of the commissioner are those provided by statute and those delegated by the corporation or the standing committee.[^cite_note-251]

In 2014, The Brihanmumbai Municipal Corporation was ranked ninth out of 21 cities for best governance & administrative practices in India, scoring 3.5 out of 10, compared to the national average of 3.3.[^cite_note-252]

A brown building with a central tower and sloping roofs surrounded by trees. A grassy ground and a coconut tree are in front of it.

The [Bombay High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_High_Court) exercises jurisdiction over Maharashtra, [Goa](40010153.md), [Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dadra_and_Nagar_Haveli_and_Daman_and_Diu).

The [Mumbai Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Police) is headed by a [police commissioner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_Commissioner_of_Mumbai), who is an [Indian Police Service](451884.md) officer. The Mumbai Police is a division of the [Maharashtra Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Police), under the home ministry of the state government.[^cite_note-253] The city is divided into seven police zones and seventeen [traffic police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_traffic_police) zones,[^cite_note-trf-254] each headed by a deputy commissioner of police.[^cite_note-255] The Mumbai Traffic Police is a semi-autonomous body under the Mumbai Police, and is responsible for traffic organisation in the city. The [Mumbai Fire Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Fire_Brigade), which is under the jurisdiction of the municipal corporation, is headed by the chief fire officer, who is assisted by four deputy chief fire officers and six divisional officers.[^cite_note-trf-254] The [Mumbai Metropolitan Region Development Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metropolitan_Region_Development_Authority) is responsible for infrastructure development and planning of [Mumbai Metropolitan Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metropolitan_Region).[^cite_note-256]

Mumbai is the seat of the [Bombay High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_High_Court), which exercises jurisdiction over the states of Maharashtra and [Goa](40010153.md), and the [Union Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Territory) of [Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dadra_and_Nagar_Haveli_and_Daman_and_Diu).[^cite_note-257] Mumbai has two lower courts, the Court of Small Causes for civil matters, and the [Sessions Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sessions_Court) for criminal cases. Mumbai has a special [Terrorist and Disruptive Activities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorist_and_Disruptive_Activities_\(Prevention\)_Act) court for people accused of conspiring and abetting acts of terrorism in the city.[^cite_note-258]

### Politics

Men in traditional Indian dresses posing for a photograph

First session of the [Indian National Congress](149333.md) in Mumbai (28–31 December 1885)

The founding session of the [Indian National Congress](149333.md) (INC) was held in Mumbai in December 1885.[^cite_note-259] Its first session was held in Mumbai from 28 to 31 December 1885.[^cite_note-260] The city played host to the sessions of the INC six times during its first 50 years, and was a base for the [Indian independence movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_independence_movement) during the 20th century.[^cite_note-261]

The 1960s saw the rise of regionalist politics, and the [Shiv Sena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiv_Sena_\(1966%E2%80%932022\)) was formed on 19 June 1966 by [Bal Thackeray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bal_Thackeray) out of resentment about the marginalisation of [Marathi people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_people) in Mumbai.[^cite_note-262] Shiv Sena switched from the 'Marathi cause' to a [Hindutva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindutva) plank in 1985 and joined hands with [Bhartiya Janata Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhartiya_Janata_Party) (BJP) that year.[^cite_note-263] The Congress had dominated the politics of Mumbai from the Indian independence until the early 1980s, when the Shiv Sena won the 1985 municipal elections for the first time.[^cite_note-264]

In 1989, the BJP forged an electoral alliance with the Shiv Sena to dislodge the Congress in the [Maharashtra Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Legislative_Assembly) elections. In 1999, several members left the Congress to form the [Nationalist Congress Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Congress_Party) (NCP) but later allied with the Congress to form the [Democratic Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Front_\(India\)).[^cite_note-265] Other parties such as [Maharashtra Navnirman Sena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Navnirman_Sena), [Samajwadi Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samajwadi_Party), [Bahujan Samaj Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahujan_Samaj_Party), [All India Majlis-e-Ittehadul Muslimeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Majlis-e-Ittehadul_Muslimeen) have contested and won seats in the local body elections in the city.[^cite_note-266]

In the [Indian national elections](1435365.md) held every five years, Mumbai is represented by six parliamentary constituencies: [North](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_North_\(Lok_Sabha_constituency\)), [North West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_North_West_\(Lok_Sabha_constituency\)), [North East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_North_East_\(Lok_Sabha_constituency\)), [North Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_North_Central_\(Lok_Sabha_constituency\)), [South Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_South_Central_\(Lok_Sabha_constituency\)), and [South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_South_\(Lok_Sabha_constituency\)).[^cite_note-267] A [member of parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_Parliament,_Lok_Sabha) to the *[Lok Sabha](275456.md)*, the lower house of the [Indian Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Parliament), is elected from each of the parliamentary constituencies. In the [2019 national election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Indian_general_election), all six parliamentary constituencies were won by the BJP and Shiv Sena in alliance, each winning three seats.[^cite_note-268]



[Maharashtra Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Legislative_Assembly)

In the [Maharashtra state assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Legislative_Assembly) elections held every five years, Mumbai is represented by 36 assembly constituencies.[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270] A [member of the legislative assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_the_Legislative_Assembly_\(India\)) to the Maharashtra *[Vidhan Sabha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidhan_Sabha)* (legislative assembly) is elected from each of the assembly constituencies. In the [2024 state assembly election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Maharashtra_Legislative_Assembly_election), out of the 36 constituencies, 15 were won by the BJP, 10 by [Shiv Sena (UBT)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiv_Sena_\(UBT\)), six by the Shiv Sena, three by the Congress, and one each by the NCP and an independent.[^cite_note-271]

Elections are held every five years to elect the corporators of the BMC.[^cite_note-272] The Corporation comprises 227 directly elected Councillors representing the [24 municipal wards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Mumbai), five nominated Councillors with knowledge of municipal administration, and a ceremonial [mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Mumbai).[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275] In the [2026 municipal corporation elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Brihanmumbai_Municipal_Corporation_election), out of the 227 seats, the BJP-Shiv Sena alliance secured 118 seats, with the BJP winning 89 seats.[^cite_note-civic20102-276]

## Transport

Main article: [Transport in Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Mumbai)

### Road



The [Bandra-Worli Sea Link](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandra-Worli_Sea_Link) is amongst the [longest sea bridges in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_longest_bridges_above_water_in_India)

Mumbai is one of the four termini of the [Golden Quadrilateral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Quadrilateral) highway system.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278] The city is connected to [Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai) and [Delhi](37756.md) by [National Highway 48](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_48_\(India\)). The highways [NH-61](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_61_\(India\)) and [NH-66](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_66_\(India\)) originate in the outskirts of the city.[^cite_note-NH-279][^cite_note-280] The [Mumbai–Pune Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai%E2%80%93Pune_Expressway) opened in 2000, and was the first [expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressways_of_India) in India.[^cite_note-281] The [Mumbai–Nagpur Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai%E2%80%93Nagpur_Expressway) opened in June 2025.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284] The [Delhi–Mumbai Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi%E2%80%93Mumbai_Expressway),[^cite_note-285] is under construction from 2019, and is planned to be completed by 2027.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288] In 2022, the state government proposed the Konkan Expressway, traversing along the Konkan coast.[^cite_note-289]

Mumbai has approximately 1,900 km (1,200 mi) of public roads.[^cite_note-Autos-290] The [Eastern Freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Freeway_\(Mumbai\)), which connects South Mumbai with Thane, opened in 2013.[^cite_note-291] The [Sion Panvel Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sion_Panvel_Expressway) from [Sion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sion,_Mumbai) to [Panvel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panvel) and the [Western Express Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Express_Highway) from [Bandra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandra) to [Bhayander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhayander), are the other major arterial roads in the city.[^cite_note-292] The [Bandra-Worli Sea Link](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandra-Worli_Sea_Link) bridge, along with [Mahim Causeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahim_Causeway), links South Mumbai to the city's western suburbs.[^cite_note-293] The 21.8 km (13.5 mi) long [Mumbai Trans Harbour Link](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Trans_Harbour_Link) was inaugurated on 12 January 2024, and connects Mumbai with Navi Mumbai.[^cite_note-294] There are five [tolled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toll_plaza) entry points to the city by road.[^cite_note-295]

Mumbai's transport system is one of the most congested in the world.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] As of March 2014, Mumbai had about 721,000 private vehicles.[^cite_note-298] Black-and-yellow meter [taxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxicabs), and [auto rickshaws](593765.md) serve are available for hire.[^cite_note-Bus-299] Auto rickshaws are allowed to operate only in suburban areas, while taxis are allowed to operate throughout the city, but work mostly in the City district.[^cite_note-Taxi-300] Taxis and the lower-cost auto rickshaws are legally required to run on [compressed natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compressed_natural_gas).[^cite_note-Taxi-300][^cite_note-301] As of 2005, there were 56,459 black and yellow taxis[^cite_note-302] and 106,000 auto rickshaws, as of May 2013.[^cite_note-Autos-290] Ride hailing services [Ola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ola_Consumer), [Rapido](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapido_\(company\)), and [Uber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uber) operate in the city.[^cite_note-303]

#### Bus

A BEST bus on Route 56

A public bus run by [Brihanmumbai Electric Supply and Transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brihanmumbai_Electric_Supply_and_Transport)

The public bus transport system is operated by [Brihanmumbai Electric Supply and Transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brihanmumbai_Electric_Supply_and_Transport) (BEST). Buses carried over 5.5 million passengers per day in 2008, and the BEST bus services together with the Mumbai's local rail network accounted for about 88% of the passenger traffic.[^cite_note-Bus-299] The bus ridership dropped to 2.8 million in 2015.[^cite_note-304] Buses are favoured for commuting short to medium distances.[^cite_note-305] Public buses cover the entire city, and extend to parts of [Mira-Bhayandar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mira-Bhayandar), Navi Mumbai, and Thane.[^cite_note-306]

In 2011, the BEST operated 4,608 buses along 390 routes.[^cite_note-Bus-299][^cite_note-307] The fleet consists of single-decker, double-decker, vestibule, low-floor, and air-conditioned buses, and run on diesel, compressed natural gas, or [electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus) power.[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309] The earlier BEST buses were red, and based on London's [Routemaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AEC_Routemaster) buses.[^cite_note-310] BEST introduced air-conditioned buses in 1998.[^cite_note-311]

[Maharashtra State Road Transport Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_State_Road_Transport_Corporation) buses provide intercity transport connecting Mumbai with other cities of Maharashtra and nearby states.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313][^cite_note-314] The [Navi Mumbai Municipal Transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navi_Mumbai_Municipal_Transport) and [Thane Municipal Transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thane_Municipal_Transport) operate buses in Mumbai, connecting Navi Mumbai and Thane to parts of Mumbai.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316] The *Mumbai Darshan* tourist bus service covers the various [tourist attractions in the city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tourist_attractions_in_Mumbai).[^cite_note-317] Bus Rapid Transit System lanes have been planned throughout Mumbai.[^cite_note-318]

### Rail



Rail map of Mumbai

The [Mumbai Suburban Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Suburban_Railway), colloquially referred to as "locals", forms the backbone of the city's transport system.[^cite_note-Outlook-319] It is operated by the [Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Railway_zone) and [Western Railway zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Railway_zone) of the [Indian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Railways).[^cite_note-320] Mumbai's suburban rail systems carried 6.3 million passengers per day in 2007.[^cite_note-321] The suburban rail network spans 319 km (198 mi), and 2,226 train services operated daily in 2010.[^cite_note-322] The trains are often overcrowded during peak hours, with twelve-car trains with a rated capacity 1,700 passengers, carrying nearly 4,500 passengers.[^cite_note-323]

The [Mumbai Monorail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Monorail) and [Mumbai Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Metro) were inaugurated to relieve the overcrowding on the existing rail network. The Monorail operates on a single route and opened in early February 2014.[^cite_note-324] The [first line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_1_\(Mumbai_Metro\)) of the Mumbai Metro opened in June 2014.[^cite_note-325] After that, many lines of Mumbai Metro have started operating partially or fully.

Mumbai is the headquarters of two zones of the [Indian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Railways): the Central Railway headquartered at [Chhatrapati Shivaji Terminus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shivaji_Terminus), and the Western Railway headquartered at [Churchgate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Churchgate).[^cite_note-326] Mumbai is well connected with major stations across the Indian Railways network. Long-distance trains operate from Chhatrapati Shivaji Terminus, [Dadar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dadar_railway_station), [Lokmanya Tilak Terminus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lokmanya_Tilak_Terminus), [Mumbai Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Central), and [Bandra Terminus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandra_Terminus) railway stations.[^cite_note-327] As of 2026, four lines of the metro are operational and eight are planned.[^cite_note-328]
