### Air



[Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shivaji_Maharaj_International_Airport) is the second busiest airport in India

The [Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shivaji_Maharaj_International_Airport) is the main aviation hub in the city. It handled 52.8 million passengers in 2023–24, and is the second busiest airport in India in terms of passenger traffic.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330] A modernisation and upgrade plan was initiated in 2006, targeted at increasing the capacity of the airport to handle up to 40 million passengers annually,[^cite_note-331] and the new T2 terminal was opened in February 2014.[^cite_note-332]

The [Navi Mumbai International airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navi_Mumbai_International_airport), built in the [Ulwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulwe) suburb in Raigad district, opened in December 2025, and serves as the second international airport in the metropolitan region.[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334] The [Juhu Aerodrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juhu_Aerodrome) hosts the [Bombay Flying Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Flying_Club), a [heliport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heliport) operated by state-owned [Pawan Hans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawan_Hans), and handles private flights.[^cite_note-335]

### Water



[Jawaharlal Nehru Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru_Port) is the busiest port in India

Main article: [Water transport in Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_transport_in_Mumbai)

Mumbai is served by two major ports, [Mumbai Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Port) and [Jawaharlal Nehru Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru_Port), which lies just across the creek in Navi Mumbai.[^cite_note-336] Mumbai Port is a natural harbour, and has extensive wet and dry dock accommodation facilities.[^cite_note-337] Jawaharlal Nehru Port, commissioned on 26 May 1989, is the busiest major port in India,[^cite_note-338] and handles around 50% of the country's total containerised cargo.[^cite_note-339]

Local water transport in Mumbai consists of ferries, small boats, and catamarans. Ferry services are operated by government agencies and private partners.[^cite_note-340] [Hovercraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hovercraft) services plied briefly in the late 1990s between the [Gateway of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gateway_of_India) and [CBD Belapur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBD_Belapur) in Navi Mumbai, and were scrapped due to lack of adequate [infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_transport_infrastructure) and opposition from the local fishermen.[^cite_note-341] Ferries from [Ferry Wharf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferry_Wharf) in [Mazagaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazagaon) allow access to islands near the city.[^cite_note-342]

The city is also the headquarters of the [Western Naval Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Naval_Command), and an important base for the [Indian Navy](604522.md).[^cite_note-mloc-146]

## Utility services

See also: [Water sources of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_sources_of_Mumbai)



[Deonar dumping ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deonar_dumping_ground) along the [Eastern Express Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Express_Highway)

The municipal corporation supplies potable water to the city drawn from six lakes and water bodies in and around the city.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344] The Tansa lake supplies water to the western suburbs and parts of the City district.[^cite_note-345] The water drawn from the lake is filtered at [Bhandup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhandup), which has Asia's largest water filtration plant.[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348] The water is supplied to the filtering facility using underground water tunnels.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350] In 2009, the city required a daily supply of 3.5 billion litres of water, of which 700 million litres is lost by way of water thefts, illegal connections and leakages.[^cite_note-351]

In 2005, the city generated a refuse of 7,800 t (7,700 long tons; 8,600 short tons), of which 40 t (39 long tons; 44 short tons) is [plastic waste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plastic_pollution),[^cite_note-352] and is transported to dumping grounds in [Gorai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gorai) in the northwest, [Mulund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulund) in the northeast, and to the [Deonar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deonar_dumping_ground) in the east.[^cite_note-353] Sewage treatment is carried out at [Worli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worli) and [Bandra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandra), and disposed of by two independent marine outfalls of 3.4 km (2.1 mi) and 3.7 km (2.3 mi) at Bandra and Worli respectively.[^cite_note-354]

Electricity is distributed by BEST in the City district, and by Maharashtra State Electricity Distribution Company ([Mahavitaran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahavitaran)), [Adani Transmission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adani_Transmission), and [Tata Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Power) in the suburbs.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356] Majority of the power supply cables are [underground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Undergrounding), to reduce pilferage, thefts and other losses.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] Cooking gas is supplied in the form of [Liquefied petroleum gas](361384.md) cylinders sold by various state-owned oil companies, and pieped [natural gas](832128.md) supplied by Mahanagar Gas Limited.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360]

The state-owned [MTNL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTNL), which held a monopoly over fixed line and cellular services up until 2000, and provides fixed line as well as mobile [WLL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wireless_local_loop) services.[^cite_note-361] Mobile phone coverage is extensive, and the main service providers include [Bharti Airtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharti_Airtel), [Reliance Jio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jio), MTNL, and [Vodafone Idea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Idea).[^cite_note-362] Mumbai, along with the area served by [telephone exchanges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telephone_exchange) in Navi Mumbai and [Kalyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalyan) is classified as a metro telecom circle.[^cite_note-363] Various operators provide broadband internet and wireless internet access, and as of 2014, Mumbai had the highest number of internet users in India with 16.4 million users.[^cite_note-364]

## Education

See also: [List of educational institutions in Mumbai § Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_educational_institutions_in_Mumbai#Schools), and [List of colleges in Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_in_Mumbai)

### Schools

Schools in Mumbai include municipal schools run by the municipal corporation, government aided, and private schools.[^cite_note-365] The schools usually use Marathi or English as the primary language of instruction.[^cite_note-366] As of 2026, the BMC operated 1,135 schools which provided free education to nearly 293,000 students. The schools taught in eight languages (Marathi, Hindu, Gujarati, Urdu, English, [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language), [Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_language) and [Kannada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada_language)).[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368]

### Higher education

Under the [10+2+3 plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/10+2+3_plan), students complete ten years of schooling and then two years in [junior college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_college), where they select one of three streams: arts, commerce, or science.[^cite_note-369] This is followed by either a degree course in any of three disciplines, or a professional degree course, such as engineering, law, and medicine.[^cite_note-370]

Most colleges are affiliated with the [University of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Mumbai), established in 1857, and is one of the premier universities in India.[^cite_note-Old-371][^cite_note-372] It was ranked 41 among the top 50 engineering schools by *[Business Insider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Insider)* in 2012,[^cite_note-373] fifth in the list of best universities in India by *[India Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_Today)* in 2013,[^cite_note-374] and 10th among the top Universities of India in 2013 in the [QS World University Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QS_World_University_Rankings).[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376] The [Wilson College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilson_College,_Mumbai) was the first college in the city, established in 1832. The [Grant Government Medical College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grant_Medical_College_and_Sir_JJ_Group_of_Hospitals) was opened in 1845, the [Government Law College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Law_College,_Mumbai) in 1855, and [Elphinstone College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elphinstone_College) in 1856. The [Veermata Jijabai Technological Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veermata_Jijabai_Technological_Institute), was the first engineering institute, opened in 1887.[^cite_note-Old-371]

The [Indian Institute of Technology Bombay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IIT_Bombay),[^cite_note-377] and [Institute of Chemical Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Chemical_Technology) are amongst the major engineering schools in the city.[^cite_note-378] Mumbai is home to several prominent business schools including [IIM Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Management_Mumbai), [Jamnalal Bajaj Institute of Management Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamnalal_Bajaj_Institute_of_Management_Studies), [Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narsee_Monjee_Institute_of_Management_Studies), [S P Jain Institute of Management and Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S_P_Jain_Institute_of_Management_and_Research), and [Tata Institute of Social Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Institute_of_Social_Sciences).[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] The [Sir J. J. School of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_J._J._School_of_Art) is Mumbai's oldest art institution.[^cite_note-382] The city is home to the [Tata Institute of Fundamental Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Institute_of_Fundamental_Research), and the [Bhabha Atomic Research Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhabha_Atomic_Research_Centre), which operates [CIRUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIRUS) nuclear research reactor in [Trombay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trombay).[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384]

  [University of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Mumbai)

## Culture

Main article: [Mumbai culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_culture)

A white building with a triangular façade and wide stairs

[The Asiatic Society of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Asiatic_Society_of_Mumbai) is one of the oldest [public libraries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_library) in the city.



[Girgaum Chowpatty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Girgaum_Chowpatty) beach. Beaches are a popular tourist attraction in the city.

Mumbai's culture offers a blend of traditional and cosmopolitan festivals, food, entertainment, and night life. Many cultures, religions, and cuisines coexist in the city, producing varied restaurants, cinemas, theatres, sports events and museums.[^cite_note-385]

Mumbai is the birthplace of [Indian cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_cinema).[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] It has many cinemas that feature Bollywood, Marathi and Hollywood movies. The [Mumbai International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_International_Film_Festival)[^cite_note-388] and the award ceremony of the [Filmfare Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filmfare_Awards), the oldest and prominent film awards given for Hindi film industry in India, are held in Mumbai.[^cite_note-389] Despite most of the professional theatre groups that formed during the [British Raj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj) having disbanded by the 1950s, Mumbai has developed a thriving "theatre movement" tradition in Marathi, Hindi, English, and other regional languages.[^cite_note-multitheater-390][^cite_note-391]

Contemporary art is featured in both government-funded art spaces and private commercial galleries. The government-funded institutions include the [Jehangir Art Gallery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehangir_Art_Gallery) and the [National Gallery of Modern Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Modern_Art). Built in 1833, the [Asiatic Society of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asiatic_Society_of_Bombay) is one of the oldest [public libraries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_library) in the city.[^cite_note-392] The [Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj Vastu Sangrahalaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shivaji_Maharaj_Vastu_Sangrahalaya) (formerly The Prince of Wales Museum) is a renowned museum in [South Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Mumbai) which houses rare ancient exhibits of Indian history.[^cite_note-393]

Mumbai has a zoo named [Jijamata Udyaan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jijamata_Udyaan) (formerly Victoria Gardens), which also harbours a garden. The literary traditions of the city have been highlighted internationally by [Booker Prize](4446.md) winners such as [Salman Rushdie](28722.md) and [Aravind Adiga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aravind_Adiga). [Marathi literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_literature) has been modernised in the works of Mumbai-based authors such as Mohan Apte, [Anant Kanekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anant_Kanekar), and [Gangadhar Gadgil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangadhar_Gadgil), and is promoted through an annual [Sahitya Akademi Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahitya_Akademi_Award), a literary honour bestowed by India's [National Academy of Letters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahitya_Akademi).[^cite_note-394]

Mumbai residents celebrate both Western and [Indian festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_festivals). [Ganesh Chaturthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganesh_Chaturthi) is the biggest and most important festival of Mumbai; there are almost 5000 [Ganpati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganpati) Pandals set up in the city for the celebrations. Other festivals like [Diwali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diwali), [Holi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holi), [Navratri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navratri), [Christmas](6237.md), [Rakshabandhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rakshabandhan), [Makar Sankranti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makar_Sankranti), [Dussera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navrati), [Eid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_holidays), [Durga Puja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durga_Puja), [Ram Navami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ram_Navami), [Shiv Jayanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiv_Jayanti) and [Maha Shivratri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maha_Shivratri) are some of the popular festivals in the city. The [Kala Ghoda Arts Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kala_Ghoda_Arts_Festival) is an exhibition that encapsulates works of artists in the fields of music, dance, theatre and films.[^cite_note-395]

The Banganga Festival is a two-day music festival, held annually in the month of January, which is organised by the [Maharashtra Tourism Development Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Tourism_Development_Corporation) (MTDC) at the historic [Banganga Tank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banganga_Tank) in Mumbai. According to a legend, it is associated with [Rama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rama) and [Lakshmana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakshmana) of the Sanskrit epic [Ramayana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramayana).[^cite_note-396] Similarly it is also linked to [Parashurama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parashurama) in Hinduism. On the bank of the Banganga tank, there is a temple known as [Parshuram Mandir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parshuram_Mandir,_Walkeshwar) dedicated to Parshuram.[^cite_note-MTDCpullout-397][^cite_note-MTDCBanganga-398][^cite_note-399]



View of [Parshuram Mandir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parshuram_Mandir,_Walkeshwar) from the Banganga tank in the Walkeshwar area of Malabar Hill in South Mumbai

The Elephanta Festival—celebrated every February on the [Elephanta Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elephanta_Island)—is dedicated to classical Indian dance and music and attracts performers from across the country.[^cite_note-MTDCpullout-397][^cite_note-MTDCElephanta-400] Public holidays specific to the city and the state include [Maharashtra Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Day) on 1 May, to celebrate the formation of Maharashtra state on 1 May 1960,[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] and [Gudi Padwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gudi_Padwa) which is the New Year's Day for [Marathi people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi_people).

[Beaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Mumbai_beaches) are a major tourist attraction in the city. The major beaches in Mumbai are [Girgaum Chowpatty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Girgaum_Chowpatty), [Juhu Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juhu_Beach), Dadar Chowpatty, Gorai Beach, [Marve Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marve_Beach), Versova Beach, Madh Beach, [Aksa Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aksa_Beach) and [Manori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manori) Beach.[^cite_note-403] Most of the beaches are unfit for swimming, except Girgaum Chowpatty and Juhu Beach.[^cite_note-404] Essel World is a theme park and amusement centre situated close to Gorai Beach,[^cite_note-405] and includes Asia's largest theme water park, Water Kingdom.[^cite_note-406] [Adlabs Imagica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adlabs_Imagica) opened in April 2013 is located near the city of Khopoli off the [Mumbai-Pune Expressway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai-Pune_Expressway).[^cite_note-founderimagica-407]

## Architecture

Main article: [Architecture of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Mumbai)

See also: [Heritage structures in Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heritage_structures_in_Mumbai) and [List of tallest buildings in Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Mumbai)



The skyline of [Worli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worli) and [Dadar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dadar) viewed from [Bandra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandra)

The architecture of the city is a blend of [Gothic Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_Revival), [Indo-Saracenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Saracenic), [Art Deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Deco), and other contemporary styles. Most of the buildings during the British period, such as the [Victoria Terminus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Terminus) and [University of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Mumbai), were built in Gothic Revival style.[^cite_note-408] Their architectural features include a variety of European influences such as German gables, Dutch roofs, Swiss timbering, Romance arches, Tudor casements, and traditional Indian features.[^cite_note-Profile-409] There are also a few Indo-Saracenic styled buildings such as the [Gateway of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gateway_of_India).[^cite_note-410] Art Deco styled landmarks can be found along [Marine Drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_Drive,_Mumbai) and west of the [Oval Maidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oval_Maidan). Mumbai has the second highest number of Art Deco buildings in the world after [Miami](53846.md). In the newer suburbs, modern buildings dominate the landscape. Mumbai has by far the highest number of [skyscrapers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skyscrapers) in India, with 247 existing skyscrapers and 200-300 under construction as of 2026.

The Mumbai Heritage Conservation Committee (MHCC), established in 1995, formulates special regulations and by-laws to assist in the conservation of the city's heritage structures. Mumbai has three [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Sites), the Chhatrapati Shivaji Terminus, the [Elephanta Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elephanta_Caves) and the [Victorian and Art Deco Ensemble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Victorian_and_Art_Deco_Ensemble_of_Mumbai).[^cite_note-411] In the south of Mumbai, there are colonial-era buildings and Soviet-style offices.[^cite_note-economist-412] In the east are factories and some slums. On the West coast are former-textile mills being demolished and skyscrapers built on top. There are 237 buildings taller than 100 m (330 ft), compared with 327 in Shanghai and 855 in New York.[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-economist-412]

### Cityscape



Skyline across [Back Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Back_Bay_\(Mumbai\))

Mumbai's cityscape consists of several tall buildings and structures, most of which were built in the 21st century. There was a lull in construction from the mid-1990s, after which construction projects began taking the skyline upwards, with a major acceleration since 2000, when the [Lower Parel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parel) area began developing.[^cite_note-414] Amongst the tall buildings, Mumbai has more residential buildings rather than commercial, and limited land resources and an exponential increase in urban population are the primary reasons for Mumbai's vertical growth compared to other Indian cities.[^cite_note-415] As of 2023, about 77% of the tallest buildings in India are in Mumbai, and the city commands premium real estate prices compared to other cities in India.[^cite_note-416] As of June 2023, Mumbai has a total of around 250 tall buildings out of which more than 100 are completed, and more than 90 were under construction.[^cite_note-417]

## Food

This section is an excerpt from [Street food of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_food_of_Mumbai).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Street_food_of_Mumbai\&action=edit)]

[Street food of Mumbai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_food_of_Mumbai) is the food sold by [hawkers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawker_\(trade\)) from portable market stalls in Mumbai. Although street food is common all over India, Mumbai is known for its street food and is a strong characteristic of the city.[^cite_note-Street_food_of_Mumbai_da04-418][^cite_note-Street_food_of_Mumbai_jg07-419] It has this reputation because people from all [economic classes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_class) eat on the roadside almost round the clock, and it is sometimes felt that the taste of street food is better than that of restaurants in the city.[^cite_note-Street_food_of_Mumbai_nyt09-420][^cite_note-Street_food_of_Mumbai_td10-421][^cite_note-Street_food_of_Mumbai_dnaj09a-422] Street food vendors are credited by some for developing the city's [food culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_culture).[^cite_note-Street_food_of_Mumbai_jsa08-423][^cite_note-Street_food_of_Mumbai_dc11-424] Street food is relatively inexpensive as compared to restaurants, and vendors tend to be clustered around crowded areas such as [colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College) and [railway stations](26161590.md).[^cite_note-Street_food_of_Mumbai_nyt09-420] Mumbai's street food ranked No.10 in the Time Out's World's Best Street Food Cities 2026 list. Mumbai was amongst 3 Indian cities which made it to the list, the other two being Jaipur at No.15 and Delhi at No. 19.[^cite_note-425]

## Media

See also: [List of Mumbai radio stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Mumbai_radio_stations)



Plaque at [Film City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_City,_Mumbai) entrance

[Bollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bollywood), the Hindi film industry based in Mumbai, produces around 150–200 films every year.[^cite_note-426] The name Bollywood is a blend of Bombay and [Hollywood](418334.md).[^cite_note-427] The 2000s saw a growth in Bollywood's popularity overseas. This led filmmaking to new heights in terms of quality, cinematography and innovative story lines as well as technical advances such as special effects and animation.[^cite_note-428] Studios in Goregaon, including [Film City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_City,_Mumbai), are the location for most movie sets.[^cite_note-429]



*[The Times of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_India)*'s first office is opposite the [Chhatrapati Shivaji Terminus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shivaji_Terminus) where it was founded.

[^cite_note-televisionpoint2006-430]

Mumbai has numerous [newspaper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_newspapers) publications, television and radio stations. Marathi dailies enjoy the maximum readership share in the city and the top [Marathi-language newspapers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathi-language_newspapers) are *[Maharashtra Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharashtra_Times)*, *[Navakaal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nava_Kaal)*, *[Lokmat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lokmat)*, *[Loksatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loksatta_\(newspaper\))*, *Mumbai Chaufer*, *[Saamana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saamana)* and *[Sakaal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sakaal)*.[^cite_note-431] English-language newspapers published in Mumbai include *[The Times of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_India)*, *[Hindustan Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustan_Times)*, and *[The Indian Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Indian_Express)*.[^cite_note-432] Asia's oldest newspaper, *[Bombay Samachar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Samachar)*, has been published in Gujarati since 1822.[^cite_note-433] *Bombay Durpan*, the first Marathi newspaper, was founded by [Balshastri Jambhekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balshastri_Jambhekar) in 1832.[^cite_note-PubliclifeMSG-434]

The metropolis is the hub of many international media corporations, with many news channels and print publications. The national television broadcaster, [Doordarshan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doordarshan), provides two free terrestrial channels, while three main cable networks serve most households.[^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436] Prominent DTH entertainment services in Mumbai include [Dish TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dish_TV) and [Tata Sky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Sky).[^cite_note-437]

There are twelve radio stations in Mumbai, with nine broadcasting on the [FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frequency_modulation) band, and three [All India Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Radio) stations broadcasting on the [AM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amplitude_modulation) band.[^cite_note-438] Mumbai has access to [Commercial radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_broadcasting) providers such as [Sirius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirius_Satellite_Radio). The [Conditional Access System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conditional_Access_System) started by the [Union Government](553883.md) in 2006 met a poor response in Mumbai due to competition from its sister technology [Direct-to-Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_broadcast_satellite) transmission service.[^cite_note-439]

## Sports



[Wankhede Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wankhede_Stadium)



[Mumbai Football Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Football_Arena)

[Cricket](25675557.md) is more popular than any other sport in Mumbai. It is home to the [Board of Control for Cricket in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Board_of_Control_for_Cricket_in_India) (BCCI)[^cite_note-440] and [Indian Premier League](13260340.md) (IPL).[^cite_note-441] Mumbai's [first-class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First-class_cricket) team [Mumbai cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_cricket_team) has won 41 [Ranji Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranji_Trophy) titles, the most by any team.[^cite_note-442] The city based [Mumbai Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Indians) compete in the Indian Premier League. Mumbai has two international cricket stadiums, the [Wankhede Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wankhede_Stadium) and the [Brabourne Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brabourne_Stadium). The first cricket test match in India was played in Mumbai at the [Bombay Gymkhana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Gymkhana).[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444] The biggest cricketing event to be staged in the city so far is the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Cricket_World_Cup_Final) of the [2011 ICC Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_ICC_Cricket_World_Cup) which was played at the Wankhede Stadium. Mumbai and [London](17867.md) are the only two cities to have hosted both a World Cup final and the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_ICC_Champions_Trophy_Final) of an [ICC Champions Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICC_Champions_Trophy) which was played at the Brabourne Stadium in [2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_ICC_Champions_Trophy).[^cite_note-TSMH-445]

[Football](10568.md) is another popular sport in the city, with the [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) and the English [Premier League](11250.md) being followed widely.[^cite_note-446] The [Mumbai City FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_City_FC) of [Indian Super League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Super_League) (ISL) play their home matches at the [Mumbai Football Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Football_Arena).[^cite_note-447] While the [I-League 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I-League_2) club [Mumbai Kenkre FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Kenkre_FC) uses the [Cooperage Ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooperage_Ground) as home ground.[^cite_note-448]

Mumbai's first professional [American football](18951490.md) [franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franchise_\(sports\)),[^cite_note-449] the [Mumbai Gladiators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Gladiators), played its first season, in [Pune](164634.md), in late 2012.[^cite_note-450]

In [field hockey](10886.md), Mumbai is home to the [Mumbai Marines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Marines) and [Mumbai Magicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Magicians) in the [World Series Hockey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Series_Hockey) and [Hockey India League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hockey_India_League) respectively. Matches in the city are played at the [Mahindra Hockey Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahindra_Hockey_Stadium).[^cite_note-marines-451][^cite_note-magicians-452]

The Indian Badminton League (IBL), now known as the [Premier Badminton League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_Badminton_League) is also visiting Mumbai since its inaugural edition in 2013 when the final was held in Mumbai's [National Sports Club of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Sports_Club_of_India).[^cite_note-453]

[Rugby](25405.md) is another growing sport in Mumbai with league matches being held at the [Bombay Gymkhana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombay_Gymkhana) from June to November.[^cite_note-rugbyhost-454]

Every February, Mumbai holds [derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derby_\(horse_race\)) races at the [Mahalaxmi Racecourse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahalaxmi_Racecourse). [Mcdowell's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Breweries_Group) Derby is held in February at the [Turf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Western_India_Turf_Club) in Mumbai.[^cite_note-455] In March 2004, the Mumbai Grand Prix was part of the [F1 powerboat world championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F1_Powerboat_Racing),[^cite_note-456] and the [Force India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Force_India) F1 team car was unveiled in the city, in 2008.[^cite_note-457] In 2004, the annual [Mumbai Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Marathon) was established as a part of "[The Greatest Race on Earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Greatest_Race_on_Earth)". Mumbai had also played host to the [Kingfisher Airlines Tennis Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingfisher_Airlines_Tennis_Open), an [International Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Series_Tournaments) tournament of the [ATP World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Tennis_Professionals), in 2006 and 2007.[^cite_note-458]

Mumbai hosted the [141st IOC Session](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/141st_IOC_Session) from 15 to 17 October 2023.[^cite_note-459]

Bombay. The island seems at the time to have consisted of two parts; the one denominated Mahim, from the village of that name in the N.E. corner and the other Mumby from an idol to which a temple is still dedicated, as is known by the appellation of Mumbydevy, or the Goddess Mumby, which by Europeans has been corrupted into Bombay. The separation of the two islands would be again complete, if the dam called Breach Candy were removed, which keeps out the sea on the west face of the island.
